**Understand from Me in brief, O son of Kuntī, how a person attains meditation on** ***ātmā*****, by which he realizes** ***ātmā*****, and hear also about the conclusion of knowledge concerning the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Having worshipped Hari by one’s prescribed actions, and consequently having attained renunciation of all actions, one attains the level of meditation on *ātmā* (*siddhim*) through the mercy of the Lord. By being situated in that process, one realizes one’s *svarūpa* endowed with eight qualities (*brahma āpnoti*). Please understand from Me the process by which this happens. I will explain it in brief. I will also speak to you about the conclusion of knowledge concerning the Supreme Lord (*jñānasya parā niṣṭhā*). Hear that also.

### Text 51-53

*buddhyā viśuddhayā yukto dhṛtyātmānaṁ niyamya ca*  
*śabdādīn viṣayāṁs tyaktvā rāga-dveṣau vyudasya ca \|\|51\|\|*

*vivikta-sevī laghv-āśī yata-vāk-kāya-mānasaḥ*  
*dhyāna-yoga-paro nityaṁ vairāgyaṁ samupāśritaḥ \|\|52\|\|*

*ahaṅkāraṁ balaṁ darpaṁ kāmaṁ krodhaṁ parigraham*  
*vimucya nirmamaḥ śānto brahma-bhūyāya kalpate \|\|53\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**That person who is endowed with sattvic intelligence, with mind controlled by sattvic determination; who has given up all the sense objects such as sound; who has eliminated attachment and repulsion; who resorts to solitude, eats little, and controls speech, body and mind; who is completely absorbed in contemplating the Lord; who has taken shelter of detachment; who is free from false ego; who is devoid of impressions which increase the false ego; who is devoid of pride, lust, anger, and possessiveness—that person, free from worldly connections, realizes** ***ātmā*** **and is situated in peace.**

**Комментарий:**

These verses describe the process of realizing *ātmā*. Utilizing sattvic intelligence (*viśuddhayā buddhyā*) and similar determination (*dhṛtyā*), restricting the mind (*ātmānam niyamya*), making it suitable for *samādhi*, giving up all sense objects such as sound, rejecting at a distance (*vyudasya*) attraction and repulsion which are directed to the sense objects, living alone (*vivikta-sevī*), controlling eating (*laghvāśī*), turning voice, body and mind towards the object of meditation, constantly (*nityam*) absorbing the mind in thinking of the Lord (*dhyāna-yoga-paraḥ*), renouncing all things other than *ātmā* (*vairāgyam*), freeing oneself from identification with body (*ahaṅkāraḥ*), freeing oneself from the impressions (*vāsanās*) which increase that identification (*balam*), freeing oneself from pride (*darpaḥ*) which is caused by strengthening the false identity with the body, freeing oneself from hankering (*kāmaḥ*) for enjoyable items which have been taken away by the force of finished *prārabdha-karmas*, freeing oneself from anger when one’s possessions have been stolen by others, freed from possessing things, which is the action resulting from false identification, freeing oneself from worldly connections (*nirmamaḥ*)—a person who accomplishes this realizes the form of his *ātmā* with eight qualities (*brahma-bhūyāya kalpa*te). That person is peaceful like an ocean without waves (*śāntaḥ*).

### Text 54

*brahma-bhūtaḥ prasannātmā na śocati na kāṅkṣati*  
*samaḥ sarveṣu bhūteṣu mad-bhaktiṁ labhate parām \|\|54\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Having attained realization of** ***ātmā*****, being a pure soul, he does not lament for or desire anything except Me, and looks upon all other beings as equally inferior. He attains** ***bhakti*** **in the form of realizing Me.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse speaks of the attainment after realizing *ātmā*. Having realized his *svarūpa* with eight qualities (*brahma-bhūtaḥ*), being extremely pure (*prasannātmā*) because of the departure of desire which matures as *karma* and suffering, he thus does not lament for, or hanker after anything except Me. He considers all entities (whether high or low), other than Me, to be equal, thinking all of them to be similar to inferior objects like earth or wood. In this state, he attains the supreme form of *bhakti, bhakti*  as the *sādhya*whose general form is directly seeing Me*.* Thus, *parāṁ bhaktim* here means that He realizes Me*.* I previously mentioned this ultimate *bhakti* with the words *niṣṭhā jñānasya yā parā*: the conclusion of knowledge (realization) concerning Me (BG [18.50](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/18.50)). The word *prasanna* is used in the sense of “very pure” as in the phrase *prasanna salilā*, very pure water.

### Text 55

*bhaktyā mām abhijānāti yāvān yaś cāsmi tattvataḥ*  
*tato māṁ tattvato jñātvā viśate tad-anantaram \|\|55\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Only by devotion does a person know Me as I am. Having known Me in truth, he then enters Me.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse explains what occurs next. By supreme, pure *bhakti* to Me, he realizes (*abhijānāti*) Me—to what extent (*yāvān*) I exist (*yaḥ āsmi*) with My *svarūpa*, qualities and powers (*tattvataḥ*). Then (*tataḥ*), having realized Me in terms of form, qualities and powers (*mām jñātvā*), as I am in truth (*tattvataḥ*), by means of the same process of supreme *bhakti*, he joins Me (*viśate*) because of having that realization (*tad-anantaram*). When a person enters a city, it is perceived that he merges into the city. However, he does not become the city. This is the usage of the word *viśate*, to enter.

It should be understood that what is stated here is that *bhakti* alone is the cause of both realizing the Lord in truth, and joining Him. This is understood from the previous statement *bhaktyā tv ananyayā śakyaḥ*. (BG [11.54](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/11.54))  *Tad-anantaram* means “after that.” Therefore the sentence means “Having truly realizing My form, qualities and *vibhūtis*, only after that (*tad anantaram*), he joins Me.”

Or *tad-anantaram* can mean “taking that *bhakti* alone.” By the rule *lyab-lope karmaṇi pañcamī* (from *Vartikā* on *Aṣṭādhyāyī* 3.2.28), an ablative case can be used to indicate an omitted verb participle ending in *ya*. In this sentence *tad-anantaram,* with the ablative meaning of “from that,” is a substitute for “*tāṁ bhaktim ādāya.”* The omitted verb form is *ādāya*, and its substitution is *tad-anantaram.* The whole sentence would then mean *“*Then (*tataḥ*) *u*nderstanding Me in truth by pure *bhakti* (*māṁ tattvato jñātvā*), taking that *bhakti* alone (*tad-anantaram*), he joins Me (*viśate*).”

It is stated by the author of *Vedānta* that even in liberation *bhakti* still exists: *āprāyaṇāt tatrāpi hi dṛṣṭam*: it is seen in the scriptures (*hi dṛṣṭam*) that upto liberation (*āprāyaṇāt*), and even after liberation (*tatrāpi*), *bhakti* continues to exist. (*Vedānta-sūtra* 4.1.12)

For those who have destroyed ignorance through *bhakti*, the taste for *bhakti* increases (instead of disappearing), just as for those who take sugar candy and destroy jaundice, sugar candy becomes sweet. This is the conclusion of those who know the most secret knowledge. In this way, the method of practice (*sādhana*) and attainment (*sādhya*) has been stated for the *saniṣṭha* devotees.

### Text 56

*sarva-karmāṇy api sadā kurvāṇo mad-vyapāśrayaḥ*  
*mat-prasādād avāpnoti śāśvataṁ padam avyayam \|\|56\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**But that person who takes shelter of Me as his main work, though still performing all his prescribed duties incidentally, attains My indestructible, eternal abode, by My mercy.**

**Комментарий:**

Now the Lord describes the process for the *pariniṣṭhita* devotee in two and half verses. He who performs all his prescribed actions as a secondary interest, as much as he feels convenient (indicated by *api*), and who takes shelter of Me alone (*mad-vyapāśrayaḥ*) as his main interest, by My extreme mercy (*mat-prasādāt*) attains the eternal (*śāśvatam*) spiritual world (*padam*) composed of unchanging pure knowledge and bliss (*avyayam*). The author of *Vedānta* explains *sarvathāpi tatra vobhaya-liṅgāt*: the *pariniṣṭhita* devotee in all cases performs service to the Lord as a primary duty, because of indications to that effect in *śruti* and *smṛti*. (*Vedānta-sūtra* 3.4.34)

### Text 57

*cetasā sarva-karmāṇi mayi sannyasya mat-paraḥ*  
*buddhi-yogam upāśritya mac-cittaḥ satataṁ bhava \|\|57\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Offering all your activities to Me, making Me your only goal of life, taking shelter of** ***buddhi-yoga*****, remember Me always while doing your actions.**

**Комментарий:**

(This continues the description of the *pariniṣṭhita* devotee.) Because you have such a nature, having offered (*sannyasya*) all your prescribed activities to Me, your master, with consciousness devoid of the concept of being the doer (*cetasā*), taking Me alone as the one goal (*mat-paraḥ*), taking shelter of Me alone, taking shelter of *buddhi-yoga*, simply be conscious of Me (*mat-cittaḥ*) without interruption (*satatam*)-–even while performing your actions. In the manner that I have already explained to you before, with the words *yat karoṣi yad aśnāsi* (BG [9.27](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.27)), after having offered the activities to Me, you perform those activities, rather than doing them and then offering them to Me.

### Text 58

*mac-cittaḥ sarva-durgāṇi mat-prasādāt tariṣyasi*  
*atha cet tvam ahaṅkārān na śroṣyasi vinaṅkṣyasi \|\|58\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Fixing your consciousness on Me, you will cross over all obstacles by My mercy. If, on the other hand, you do not listen out of pride, you will perish.**

**Комментарий:**

Thus, with consciousness fixed in Me, by My mercy alone, you will cross over all the sufferings of *saṁsāra*, which is normally difficult to cross (*sarva-durgāṇi*). You should not worry about this. I, the friend of My devotee, will remove all of those sufferings, and I will give Myself to that devotee. Thus, the method of practice (*sādhana*) and attainment (*sādhya*) of the *pariniṣṭhita* devotee have been stated.

But if you do not hear what I have said because of thinking that you know what is to be done and what is not to be done (*ahaṅkārāt*), you will deviate from your goal (*vinaṅkṣyasi*). There exists no one other than I, among living beings, who knows what is to be done and what is not to be done. Only I know what is beneficial.

### Text 59

*yad ahaṅkāram āśritya na yotsya iti manyase*  
*mithyaiṣa vyavasāyas te prakṛtis tvāṁ niyokṣyati \|\|59\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If, by taking shelter of false ego, you think “I will not fight”, that resolve will be useless. My** ***prakṛti*** **will engage you in fighting.**

**Комментарий:**

“Even though fighting is my *dharma*, I do not have that inclination because I fear the sin caused by killing *brāhmaṇas* and *gurus*.”

If you take shelter of false identity (*ahaṅkāram*), thinking you know what is right and wrong, and contemplate “I will not fight,” such resolve (*vyavasāyaḥ*) will definitely not bring results (*mithyā*). *Prakṛti*, My *māyā*, transformed as the mode of passion, will engage you, who have ignored My words, in fighting anyway, which will cause killing of *gurus* and *brāhmaṇas.*

### Text 60

*svabhāva-jena kaunteya nibaddhaḥ svena karmaṇā*  
*kartuṁ necchasi yan mohāt kariṣyasy avaśo ’pi tat \|\|60\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Even if you do not want to fight out of ignorance, you will do it anyway, against your own will, as you are bound by actions arising from your nature.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord elaborates on this point. If, out of ignorance (*mohāt*), you do not desire to fight, even though it is My order, being bound by your actions as *kṣatriya* (*svena karmaṇā*) which arises from your impressions (*svabhāva-jena*), which are manifested by My material energy, you will do it anyway, against your will (*avaśaḥ*).

### Text 61

*īśvaraḥ sarva-bhūtānāṁ hṛd-deśe ’rjuna tiṣṭhati*  
*bhrāmayan sarva-bhūtāni yantrārūḍhāni māyayā \|\|61\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O Arjuna, the Lord is situated in the heart of all living entities. He engages in action all living entities, who are mounted on a machine made of** ***prakṛti.***

**Комментарий:**

Seeing that Arjuna still seemed to have the false concept of thinking himself the knower (and doer), and because of Arjuna’s inability to give that up, the Lord then teaches in a different way in two verses

O Arjuna, though you think that your knowledge is your own, you actually know that knowledge only through the Supreme Lord as the a*ntaryāmī*. That Lord is situated in the hearts of all beings from Brahmā down to the non-moving creatures, and He makes them wander about by His *śakti* (*māyayā*). These living entities are then described. They are mounted on the apparatus characterized by body, senses and *prāṇas* (*yantra ārūḍhāni*), constructed by *māyā* according to their *karmas* (*māyayā*).

A comparison is suggested using the ornament called *rūpaka* (metaphor). The Lord makes the living entities move about just as a puppeteer mounts artificial beings on wooden devices with strings attached (*yantra ārūḍhāni*) and makes them move about.

### Text 62

*tam eva śaraṇaṁ gaccha sarva-bhāvena bhārata*  
*tat-prasādāt parāṁ śāntiṁ sthānaṁ prāpsyasi śāśvatam \|\|62\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O descendent of Bharata, take refuge in the Paramātmā alone with all your facilities. By His mercy, you will attain the eternal spiritual abode.**

**Комментарий:**

Therefore, surrender to that Lord (*tam*) with body, mind and all other elements (*sarva-bhāvena*). Then what happens? You will attain the eternal place (*śāśvatam sthānam*), with absence of all suffering (*parāṁ śāntim*), the abode of the Lord glorified in the *śrutis* as *tad viṣṇoḥ paramaṁ padam.* That person (*tam*) is none other than I, your companion. This has been stated by Me before with *sarvasya cāhaṁ hṛdi sanniviṣṭaḥ*: I, Kṛṣṇa, am seated in everyone’s heart. (BG [15.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/15.15)) It has also been stated by you and accepted by the *devas* and *ṛṣis* with the statement *paraṁ brahma paraṁ dhāma*: You (Kṛṣṇa) are the supreme Brahman and the supreme form. (BG [10.12](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/10.12)) I have also shown that to you directly by manifesting the universal form. Therefore, remain fixed in My teachings.

### Text 63

*iti te jñānam ākhyātaṁ guhyād guhyataraṁ mayā*  
*vimṛśyaitad aśeṣeṇa yathecchasi tathā kuru \|\|63\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus I have explained a scripture more secret than the secret knowledge of** ***mantras*****. Considering this thoroughly, do as you wish to do.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord now concludes the scripture. Thus in the manner just spoken (*iti*), I have declared to you this knowledge (*jñānam*), the scripture of the *Gītā*, which is the means of understanding *karma, bhakti* and *jñāna. Jñāna* means “that by which something is known.” It is more secret than the scriptures which teach secret *mantras*. It should thus be kept secret. After reflecting on this scripture fully (*aśeṣena*), do as you wish. The intended meaning is “Reviewing this scripture thoroughly, your ignorance will be destroyed and you will be steadfast in My words.”

### Text 64

*sarva-guhyatamaṁ bhūyaḥ śṛṇu me paramaṁ vacaḥ*  
*iṣṭo ’si me dṛḍham iti tato vakṣyāmi te hitam \|\|64\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Again, hear from Me the highest words, the supreme secret, since you are very dear to Me and you have faith in the authority of My words. I will speak for your benefit.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord will now explain the process (*sādhana*) and attainment (*sādhya*) of the *nirapekṣa* devotee. First He praises this process of the *nirapekṣa* devotee. Among all secrets, this is the most secret (*sarva-guhyatamam*). Hear this again, for in the ninth chapter, I have already stated this (*man-manā bhava mad-bhaktaḥ*). But because it is very dear to Me, listen as I say it again at the end. It is the supreme (*paramam*) statement, the essence of the *Gītā*, which is the essence of all other scriptures. What is the reason for saying it again? You are the dearest to Me (*iṣṭaḥ*). You have faith that My words possess all authority (*dṛḍham iti*). Therefore, I will speak for your benefit, and therefore, you should act upon this. This is the implied meaning.

### Text 65

*man-manā bhava mad-bhakto mad-yājī māṁ namaskuru*  
*mām evaiṣyasi satyaṁ te pratijāne priyo ’si me \|\|65\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Being My devotee, offer your mind to Me. Offer articles to Me in worship. Offer respects to Me. I promise that you will come to Me alone without doubt, for you are most dear to Me.**

**Комментарий:**

Now the Lord gives the instruction. This has been explained before (BG [9.34](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.34)). By concentrating your mind on Me, worshipping Me and offering respects to Me, you will attain only Me, Kṛṣṇa, with human form, the son of Devakī, most affectionate to you. You will attain Me, with dark complexion, the color of the blue lotus—not the universal form with a thousand heads, not the form of Paramātmā, the size of a thumb, not Nṛsiṁha and not Varāha. Being your friend, I will give Myself to you. I make this oath to you (*te satyam*). The *Nānārtha Varga* says *satyaṁ śapatha-tathyayoḥ: satyam* means an oath or the truth*.* You should not have any doubts about this.

“But my doubt is not destroyed, because even when you made a vow, you did not return from Mathurā to Vraja.”

“Therefore I have said this after making an admission (*pratijāne*): you are dear to Me. If the resident of Mathurā, having an affectionate mind, does not cheat the one He loves, what then to speak of one He loves the most? I have great affection for he who has great affection for Me. I cannot tolerate separation from him. I have already said this with words like *priyo hi* (BG [7.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/7.17)). Therefore, have faith in My words: you will certainly attain Me.”

### Text 66

*sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁ śaraṇaṁ vraja*  
*ahaṁ tvāṁ sarva-pāpebhyo mokṣayiṣyāmi mā śucaḥ \|\|66\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Giving up all** ***dharmas*****, just surrender unto Me alone. I will deliver you from all sins. Do not worry.**

**Комментарий:**

“How is it possible for men whose hearts are contaminated with unlimited sins in the form of ancient *karmas* to perform pure *bhakti* to You, in the form of worship and respect as described in the last verse, unless those unlimited sins, being contrary to *bhakti*, are first destroyed by following prescribed *dharma* with atonements such as mortification of the body?”

Giving up completely (*parityajya*), in their very essence, all *dharmas* along with prescriptions such as austerities to atone for past sins, just surrender to Me, the Lord of all, Kṛṣṇa, who previously appeared in many forms such as Nṛsiṁha and Rāma, who is perceived through pure *bhakti*, and who destroys all desires, and even *avidyā* (the cause of the conditioned *jīva*). Surrender to Me alone (*mām ekam*) and not to others such as Śiva. I, the lord of all, worthy object of surrender (*aham*), will free you who have surrendered (*tvām*), from all past *karmas* (*sarva-pāpebhyaḥ*). The Lord shows the mutual actions to be done by Himself and the devotee.

You should not lament (*mā śucaḥ*), thinking that it is impossible for you, with short lifespan, to perform difficult, extreme austerities which will bring about the desired result of purification of the heart after a very long time. I have stated that in surrender to Me, this is not required. The *śrutis* also say this:

*na karmaṇā na prajayā dhanena tyāgenaike ’mṛtatvam ānaśuḥ*

**Not by prescribed duties or by progeny or wealth can one attain immortality. It is only gained by some, through self-surrender.** ***Mahānārāyaṇa Upaniṣad*** **86,** ***Kaivalya Upaniṣad*** **3**

The method of attaining the Lord is stated by such verses as this:

*śraddhā-bhakti-dhyāna-yogād avaiti*

**One knows the Lord by faith,** ***bhakti*** **and meditation.** ***Kaivalya Upaniṣad*** **2**

Now, if you are a *saniṣṭha-bhakta*, you perform *dharma* for purification of the heart. If you are a *pariniṣṭhita-bhakta*, you perform *dharmas* to teach others. Performance of *dharma* is required for these types of *bhakti* (but not for the *nirapekṣa* devotee). This is understood from the *śrutis*:

*tam etam veda anuvacanena brāhmaṇa vividiṣanti yajñena dānena tapasā anāśakena*

**The** ***brāhmaṇas*** **seek to know the Lord by study of the** ***Vedas*****, by sacrifice, by charity, by penance and by observing vows of fasting.** ***Bṛhad Āraṇyaka Upaniṣad*** **4.4.22**

*satyena labhyas tapasā hy eṣa ātmā*

**This Lord is attained by true knowledge arising from scriptures and constant celibacy.** ***Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad*** **3.1.5**

There is another explanation of the words *mā sucaḥ*. You should not worry because of the sin incurred by giving up your prescribed duties. This explanation however is not acceptable, since there is no sin at all incurred for rejecting *dharmas*. The *sannyāsī* renounces the fire sacrifice by the order of the *Veda*. Similarly, the surrendered soul renounces his prescribed duties by the order of the Lord, since they are unsuitable for him. Moreover, by not obeying this order of the Lord, a devotee will incur sin.

One should not try to explain this verse as follows. One should not completely give up all  *dharmas*, because there is sin involved in doing so. Instead, one should rather just give up the results of the *dharmas*. This is not acceptable however, because there is no sin involved in not performing *dharma* in this process. Therefore, the meaning is that the surrendered soul should give up all *dharmas* completely at the root.

Nor should one argue that a person must always perform his prescribed *dharma* based upon the reasoning that every one must act: *na hi kaścit kṣaṇam api* (BG [3.5](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.5))*.* This rule does not apply because, following that rule, the surrendered person is absorbed in worship of the Lord which is also action. Moreover, just as the practice of prescribed *dharmas* for the *saniṣṭha* devotee ends with his realization of *ātmā*, and the practice of *dharmas* for the *pariniṣṭhita* devotee ends with his realization of Paramātmā, so the practice of *dharmas* for the surrendered soul ends with faith (*śraddhā*) in the process of surrender. This is stated in the *Bhāgavatam, Eleventh Canto.*

*tāvat karmāṇi kurvīta na nirvidyeta yāvatā*  
*mat-kathā-śravaṇādau vā śraddhā yāvan na jāyate*

**As long as one is not satiated by fruitive activity and has not awakened his faith (*****śraddhā*****) for devotional service by** ***śravaṇaṁ kīrtanaṁ viṣṇoḥ*** **one has to act according to the regulative principles of the Vedic injunctions. SB** [**11.20.9**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/20.9)

*jñāna-niṣṭho virakto vā mad-bhakto vānapekṣakaḥ*  
*saliṅgānāśramāṁs tyaktvā cared avidhi-gocaraḥ*

**A learned transcendentalist dedicated to the cultivation of knowledge and thus detached from external objects, or My devotee who is detached even from desire for liberation—both neglect those duties based on external rituals or paraphernalia. Thus their conduct is beyond the range of rules and regulations. SB** [**11.18.28**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/18.28)

This surrender, also called *śaraṇāgati*, has six elements:

*ānukūlyasya saṅkalpaḥ prātikūlyasya varjanaṁ*  
*rakṣiṣyatīti viśvāso goptṛtve varaṇaṁ tathā*  
*ātma-nikṣepa-kārpaṇye ṣaḍ-vidhā śaraṇāgatiḥ*

**Surrender has six factors: accepting what is favorable, rejecting what is unfavorable, having faith that the Lord will protect one, accepting dependence on the Lord, placing oneself fully to the Lord, and meekness.** ***Vāyu Purāṇ*****a**

Actions pleasing to the Lord that are prescribed by the scriptures are called *ānukūlya* or favorable. Opposite actions, which are displeasing to the Lord, not prescribed by the scriptures, are called *prātikūlya* or unfavorable. *Ātma-nikṣepa* means “to place responsibility for one’s maintenance in the object of surrender, the Lord.” *Kārpaṇya* means “not arrogant.” Sometimes the line reads *nikṣepaṇam akārpaṇyam.* In that case, *akārpaṇya* means “not showing one’s miserable position to others.” The rest is clear.

### Text 67

*idaṁ te nātapaskāya nābhaktāya kadācana*  
*na cāśuśrūṣave vācyaṁ na ca māṁ yo ’bhyasūyati \|\|67\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is not to be spoken to a person lacking control of his senses, to a person without true devotion, to a person who is unwilling to hear, or to a person who envies Me, thinking that I am material.**

**Комментарий:**

In this verse the Lord instructs that the teachings of the *Gītā* which He Himself has taught should be given to qualified candidates only, and not to the unqualified.

This scripture should not be spoken by you to a person who does not have sense control (*atapaskāya*). Even if he does control the senses, it should not be given to the non-devotee (*abhaktāya*), who has no devotion to you, the teacher of the scripture, and to Me, the Supreme Lord, the maker of the scripture. And even if he controls the senses, and has devotion to you and Me, you should not teach to him if he has no desire to hear it (*aśuśrūṣave*). You should never speak it to that person who envies Me, the Supreme Lord, possessor of eternal form and qualities, to that person who attributes material form and qualities to Me.

The declension, nominative case (*yaḥ*), is used for the envious person instead of dative case which was used for the others in the list (*atapaskāya, abhaktāya, aśuśrūṣave*) to point out his particularly degraded nature. The author of *Vedānta* says *anāviṣkurvann anvayāt*: the teacher should tell the disciple not to reveal the knowledge to the unqualified person, because that is the custom. (*Vedānta-sūtra* 3.4.50)

### Text 68

*ya idaṁ paramaṁ guhyaṁ mad-bhakteṣv abhidhāsyati*  
*bhaktiṁ mayi parāṁ kṛtvā mām evaiṣyaty asaṁśayaḥ \|\|68\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**One who teaches this supreme secret to My devotees, attains the highest** ***bhakti*****, and finally attains Me without doubt.**

**Комментарий:**

The result for one who teaches this scripture is narrated. The one who teaches attains pure *bhakti* to Me, and then attains Me.

### Text 69

*na ca tasmān manuṣyeṣu kaścin me priya-kṛttamaḥ*  
*bhavitā na ca me tasmād anyaḥ priyataro bhuvi \|\|69\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Among men, there is no one who has satisfied Me as much as he, and never will there be in the future. There has never been one dearer to Me than he in this world, and there will never be one dearer in the future.**

**Комментарий:**

Among all men, there has never been one who satisfies Me (*priya-kṛttamaḥ*) as much as the *teacher* of the *Gītā*, nor will there be such a person in the future. There has been no one dearer to Me in this world in the past, and there will be no one dearer in the future.

### Text 70

*adhyeṣyate ca ya imaṁ dharmyaṁ saṁvādam āvayoḥ*  
*jñāna-yajñena tenāham iṣṭaḥ syām iti me matiḥ \|\|70\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is My conviction that he who studies this sacred conversation between the two of us will have worshipped Me through the sacrifice of knowledge.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes the results for one who *studies* the *Gītā*. I will have been worshipped (*iṣṭaḥ*) by the *jñāna-yajña* described previously in chapter four, just by the study of the *Gītā*. This is My opinion. I am easily attained by the person who studies the *Gītā.*

### Text 71

*śraddhāvān anasūyaś ca śṛṇuyād api yo naraḥ*  
*so ’pi muktaḥ śubhāḹ lokān prāpnuyāt puṇya-karmaṇām \|\|71\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**And that person, who, being full of faith, without envy, just hears this teaching will become free of sin and attain the auspicious world of devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes the result for the *hearer* of the *Gītā.* He who hears with faith alone, without envy-not finding fault in it by thinking “What is the purpose of his reciting this work?” or “Why does he recite an inaccurate text in a loud voice?” becomes free of all sins, and will attain the planets of those who perform  *aśvamedha* sacrifices, or the planets of the devotees such as Dhruvaloka, or the various Vaikuṇṭha planets (*śubhāḹ lokān puṇya-karmaṇām).*

### Text 72

*kaccid etac chrutaṁ pārtha tvayaikāgreṇa cetasā*  
*kaccid ajñāna-sammohaḥ praṇaṣṭas te dhanañjaya \|\|72\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O son of Pṛthā, have you heard this with close attention? O conqueror of wealth, have your ignorance and bewilderment been destroyed?**

**Комментарий:**

Thus, the scripture and the glorification of speaking, studying and hearing it have been spoken. The Lord now asks about the comprehension of the meaning of the scripture and the realization of the scripture. *Kaccid* is an indeclinable particle indicating a question. If you have not completely understood, then I will again teach it. That is the intention of the question.

### Text 73

*arjuna uvāca*  
*naṣṭo mohaḥ smṛtir labdhā tvat-prasādān mayācyuta*  
*sthito ’smi gata-sandehaḥ kariṣye vacanaṁ tava \|\|73\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna said: My delusion has been destroyed, and my correct vision has been restored by Your mercy, O Acyuta. I remain without doubts, and will follow Your instructions.**

**Комментарий:**

Being asked by Kṛṣṇa, Arjuna answers that he has understood, by reciting the results. Ignorance (*mohaḥ*), the opposite of knowledge, has disappeared, by Your mercy alone. I have understood correctly the nature of things (*smṛtiḥ labdhā*). Thus, I have now had all my doubts cleared. I will follow Your instructions.

What he has said is this. All the living entities such as *devas* and men have the conception of being their bodies, and being independent in their actions. Men worship the *devas*, and the *devas* bestow on men what they desire. But who is the real Lord? I previously had the illusion, the opposite of knowledge, that *prakṛti* is the cause of the world. In contrast to that, I thought the Lord was without qualities and form, and was indifferent to men’s affairs. That illusion has been destroyed by the teachings acquired from You.

I have now true knowledge of that Lord who is the famous possessor of form and powers, a form of knowledge and bliss; who is an ocean of good qualities such as omniscience, omnipotence, and *satya-saṅkalpa*; who is the friend of His devotees, though the Lord of all; who creates the variegated bodies in the universe according to the *karmas* of the *jīvas* just by His will, through His energies of *jīva*, time and *prakṛti*; who treasures the selfless devotee to whom He gives even Himself. And I understand that the Lord is You, the son of Vasudeva, situated as my friend.

Therefore, I surrender to You. You will never abandon me. This doubt has been destroyed. If You have Your goal to achieve of ridding the earth of its burden, and You want me who have surrendered to You to do it, then I will obey Your words. Thus, Arjuna rose with bow in hand.

### Text 74

*sañjaya uvāca*  
*ity ahaṁ vāsudevasya pārthasya ca mahātmanaḥ*  
*saṁvādam imam aśrauṣam adbhutaṁ roma-harṣaṇam \|\|74\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Sañjaya said: Thus I have heard this astonishing conversation between Vāsudeva and the son of Pṛthā, a great devotee of the Lord. It has caused my hairs to stand on end.**

**Комментарий:**

The scripture is concluded. Sañjaya speaks to Dhṛtarāṣṭra to finish the story. This discourse is astonishing (*adbhutam*) for the consciousness, because it has never appeared before among men. It has caused my body hair to stand on end (*roma-harṣaṇam*).

### Text 75

*vyāsa-prasādāc chrutavān etad guhyam ahaṁ param*  
*yogaṁ yogeśvarāt kṛṣṇāt sākṣāt kathayataḥ svayam \|\|75\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**By the mercy of Vyāsa, I have heard this secret of** ***bhakti-yoga*** **which reveals the Supreme Lord, directly from Kṛṣṇa, the controller of all beings, who is friendly to all living beings, while He personally spoke it.**

**Комментарий:**

In this verse, Sañjaya speaks of his own qualification for hearing the conversation that just took place. By the mercy of Vyāsa, attaining a form with transcendental eyes and ears given by him, I have heard this secret. What is this secret? It is the *yoga—karma-yoga, jñāna-yoga* and *bhakti-yoga*—which manifests the supreme entity (*param*). I have heard it from the controller (*īśvara*) of all beings, who has a natural fellowship with all living entities such as *devas* and men (*yoga*). I have heard it from the original form (*svayam*), Kṛṣṇa, from His own mouth as He spoke it (*kathayataḥ*), not from another person indirectly. Sañjaya celebrates his own good fortune.

### Text 76

*rājan saṁsmṛtya saṁsmṛtya saṁvādam imam adbhutam*  
*keśavārjunayoḥ puṇyaṁ hṛṣyāmi ca muhur muhuḥ \|\|76\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O King, remembering this wonderful, purifying conversation between Keśava and Arjuna again and again, my hairs are standing on end at every moment.**

**Комментарий:**

O king, O Dhṛtarāṣṭra, remembering this wondrous conversation which destroys all faults of the hearer, and destroys even *avidyā*—the cause of our existence in this world (*puṇyam*), at every moment (*muhur muhuḥ*) my hairs are standing on end.

### Text 77

*tac ca saṁsmṛtya saṁsmṛtya rūpam aty-adbhutaṁ hareḥ*  
*vismayo me mahān rājan hṛṣyāmi ca punaḥ punaḥ \|\|77\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O King, remembering also, again and again, that amazing form of Hari, the universal form, I am filled with wonder, and my hairs stand on end again and again.**

**Комментарий:**

*Tat* here refers to the universal form which was shown to Arjuna.

### Text 78

*yatra yogeśvaraḥ kṛṣṇo yatra pārtho dhanur-dharaḥ*  
*tatra śrīr vijayo bhūtir dhruvā nītir matir mama \|\|78\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Where there is Kṛṣṇa, the controller, the friend of all beings, and where there is Arjuna, the holder of the bow, there will be unfailing prosperity, victory, ever-increasing wealth, and righteousness. That is my conviction.**

**Комментарий:**

This being so, give up the desire for the victory of your sons. Where there is the master who is related to all beings (*yogeśvāraḥ*, explained above), who brings into motion and maintains the forms of all other living entities just by His will, where there is Kṛṣṇa, the son of Vasudeva, who takes the role of a helper even as the charioteer of Arjuna, and where there is Arjuna, the son of Prthā, Kṛṣṇa’s father’s sister, who is the *avatāra* called Nara, who is the holder of the bow Gāṇḍīva which cannot be cut (*dhanur-dharaḥ*)—at that place alone, where Kṛṣṇa and Arjuna are situated, siding with Yudhiṣṭhira, there will also be secured the wealth of kings (*śrī*), victory (*vijaya*)—great superiority causing the defeat of the enemy, the constant increase of that royal wealth (*bhūtiḥ*), and the permanent (*dhruvā*) sense of justice (*nītiḥ*).

One should not worry that this scripture is favoring war. It is not so. The instructions are *man-manā bhava mad-bhakta* and *sarva-dharmān parityajya.* It is clear that the prescribed actions according to the four *varṇas* and *āśrama*s (including fighting) have been delineated for purification of the heart, and for teaching the populace.

There are many methods, but among them, surrender (*prapatti*), along with the attitude of being a servant, quickly pleases Viṣṇu. This is the conclusion of the eighteenth chapter.

In the *Gītā*, the supreme truth Kṛṣṇa, filled with all spiritual qualities, who drunk the breast milk of Yaśodā, and who became the charioteer of Arjuna, is glorified.

Attaining the fulfillment of my desires, I immersed myself in the milk ocean of the *Gītā*, but I have not been able to lift the jewel of wonderful meanings out of the ocean, though I have understood them to some degree. In this way, my master, the joyful son of Nanda, with great delight, shows His affection for me.

With care, Vidyābhūṣaṇa has attended upon this commentary called *Śrīmad Gītā Bhūṣaṇa*. O devotees who are greedy for the sweetness of *prema* of Śrī Govinda and who are filled with mercy, please purify this work.
